Empty 0x1 cell. Need to manually define what it becomes in Python.\n2. 1x1 cell. Will be directly read as non-iterables such as str, int, float, etc. Need to manually convert it to an iterable with length 1.\n3. Nx1 cell (N >= 2). Usually it's fine to read and directly store it in Python.\n\nComment: in case 2, when using isinstance(data, data_type), if data could be a number, consider the possibility of data being either float or int, i.e. use\n    if isinstance(data, float) or isinstance(data, int):\n        do something\n\"\"\"\n\nplt.rcParams['pdf.fonttype'] = 42\nplt.rcParams['ps.fonttype'] = 42\n\n\ndef sliding_histogram(spikeTimes, begin_time, end_time, bin_width, stride, rate=True):\n    '''\n    Calculates the number of spikes for each unit in each sliding bin of width bin_width, strided by stride.\n    begin_time and end_time are treated as the lower- and upper- bounds for bin centers.\n    if rate=True, calculate firing rates instead of bin spikes.\n    '''\n    bin_begin_time = begin_time\n    bin_end_time = end_time\n    # This is to deal with cases where for e.g. (bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride is actually 43 but evaluates to 42.999999... due to numerical errors in floating-point arithmetic.\n    if np.allclose((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride, math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1):\n        n_bins =  math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+2\n    else:\n        n_bins = math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1\n    binCenters = bin_begin_time + np.arange(n_bins)*stride\n\n    binIntervals = np.vstack((binCenters-bin_width/2, binCenters+bin_width/2)).T\n\n    # Count number of spikes for each sliding bin\n    binSpikes = np.asarray([[[np.sum(np.all([trial>=binInt[0], trial<binInt[1]], axis=0)) for binInt in binIntervals] for trial in unit]\n        for unit in spikeTimes]).swapaxes(0,-1)\n\n    if rate:\n        return binCenters, binSpikes/float(bin_width) # get firing rates\n    return binCenters, binSpikes\n\n\ndef process_one_sess(data_folder, file_path_list, bw, stride, begin_time=-3.0, end_time=3.5, save_folder=os.path.join(os.pardir, 'data_processed'), qc_mode='classifier'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process all probe files in one session.\n\n    Args:\n        data_folder (str): the folder containing all data including QC neuron ID files.\n        file_path_list: list of full file paths of all probes in this session.\n        bw (float): in sec.\n        stride (float): in sec.\n        qc_mode (str): 'classifier' or 'old'.\n    \"\"\"\n    print(qc_mode)\n    if qc_mode == 'classifier':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'goodunits')\n        print(qc_folder)\n    elif qc_mode == 'old':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'DJ_GoodUnitsIdx_14regions_old_qc')\n    else:\n        raise ValueError('Wrong qc_mode! model.fit(X_train, y_train)\n   237→        y_pred = model.predict(X_test)\n   238→        r2_scores = r2_score(y_test, y_pred, multioutput='raw_values')\n   239→\n   240→ 247→\n   248→def temporal_alignment_embed_and_ephys(ephys_tt,embed_tt, embed_vecs, fr, dt = 0.0034):\n   249→    '''Align the embeding vectors and the firing rates in time.\n   250→    \n   251→    This assumes that the dt is the same for both datasets.\n   252→\n   253→    Parameters\n   254→    ----------\n   255→    ephys_tt : np.ndarray\n   256→        The time stamps for the ephys data.\n   257→    embed_tt : np.ndarray\n   258→        The time stamps for the embedding vectors.\n   259→    embed_vecs : np.ndarray [time, trial, embedding_dim]\n   260→        The embedding vectors.\n   261→    fr : np.ndarray [time, trial, neuron]\n   262→        The firing rates.\n   263→    dt : float, optional\n   264→        The time step. for the sample period (old method).\n   345→    delay_r2_old : np.ndarray\n   346→        The average R2 scores for the delay period (old method).\n   347→    response_r2_old : np.ndarray\n   348→        The average R2 scores for the response period (old method).\n   349→\n   350→    '''\n   351→    dt = 0.0034\n   352→    tt = r2_dict['tt']\n   353→    r2_scores = r2_dict['r2_scores']\n   354→    y_test = r2_dict['y_test']\n   355→    y_pred = r2_dict['y_pred']\n   356→\n   357→    sample_mask = (tt >= sample_period[0]) * (tt < sample_period[1])\n   358→    sample_mask *= (tt >= (sample_period[0]-timeshift*dt)) * (tt < (sample_period[1]-timeshift*dt))\n   359→    delay_mask = (tt >= delay_period[0]) * (tt < delay_period[1])\n   360→    delay_mask *= (tt >= (delay_period[0]-timeshift*dt)) * (tt < (delay_period[1]-timeshift*dt))\n   361→    response_mask = (tt >= response_period[0]) * (tt < response_period[1])\n   362→    response_mask *= (tt >= Otherwise, returns the difference in means. \n   505→        The default is True.\n   506→\n   507→    Returns\n   508→    -------\n   509→    selectivity : np.ndarray\n   510→        The selectivity for each neuron.\n   511→    '''\n   512→    mean_R = fr_R.mean(axis = 0)\n   513→    mean_L = fr_L.mean(axis = 0)\n   514→    std_R = fr_R.std(axis = 0)\n   515→    std_L = fr_L.std(axis = 0)\n   516→    if normalize:\n   517→        selectivity = (mean_R - mean_L) / np.sqrt((std_R**2 + std_L**2) / 2)\n   518→    else:\n   519→        selectivity = mean_R - mean_L\n   520→    return selectivity\n   521→\n   522→def calculate_auc(fr_R, fr_L, cv=5):\n   523→    '''\n   524→    Returns the AUC of each neuron.\n   525→\n   526→    Parameters\n   527→    ----------\n   528→    fr_R : np.ndarray\n   529→        The firing rates for the right trials [trials,neurons].\n   530→    fr_L : np.ndarray\n   531→        The firing rates for the left trials [trials,neurons].\n   532→    cv : int, optional\n   533→        The number of folds for cross-validation. The default is 5.\n   534→\n   535→    Returns\n   536→    -------\n   537→    auc : np.ndarray\n   538→        The AUC for each neuron.\n   539→    '''\n   540→    from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\n   541→    from sklearn.model_selection import StratifiedKFold, GridSearchCV\n   542→    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\n   543→    import numpy as np\n   544→\n   545→    # Prepare data for the i-th neuron\n   553→        y = np.concatenate([np.ones(fr_R.shape[0]), np.zeros(fr_L.shape[0])])\n   554→        X = np.concatenate([fr_R[:, i], fr_L[:, i]]).reshape(-1, 1)\n   555→\n   556→        #    Parameters\n    ----------\n    fr : np.ndarray\n        The firing rates [time,trial,neuron].\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    mean_corr : np.ndarray\n        Mean correlation with the trial [neuron]\n    trial_to_trial_var : np.ndarray\n        Time averaged trial to trial variance. [neuron]\n    '''\n    trial_avg = fr.mean(axis = 1)\n    mean_corr = np.zeros(fr.shape[-1])\n    for i in range(fr.shape[-1]):\n        mean_corr[i] = np.mean([np.corrcoef(trial_avg[:,i], fr[:,j,i])[0][1] for j in range(fr.shape[1])])\n    \n    trial_to_trial_var = np.mean(np.var(fr, axis = 1), axis = 0)\n    return mean_corr, trial_to_trial_var\n\n\ndef calculate_selectivity(fr_R,fr_L, normalize = True):\n    '''\n    Returns the selectivity of each neuron.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    fr_R : np.ndarray\n        The firing rates for the right trials [trials,neurons].\n    fr_L : np.ndarray\n        The firing rates for the left trials [trials,neurons].\n    normalize : bool, optional\n        If True, normalizes the selectivity by the standard deviations,\n        resulting in d'. The default is 5.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    auc : np.ndarray\n        The AUC for each neuron.\n    '''\n    from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\n    from sklearn.model_selection import StratifiedKFold, GridSearchCV\n    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\n    import numpy as np\n\n    # Initialize AUC array\n    auc = np.zeros(fr_R.shape[1])\n\n    # Define parameter grid for logistic regression\n    param_grid = {'C': [0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 100]}\n\n    for i in range(fr_R.shape[1]):\n        # Prepare data for the i-th neuron\n        y = np.concatenate([np.ones(fr_R.shape[0]), np.zeros(fr_L.shape[0])])\n        X = np.concatenate([fr_R[:, i], fr_L[:, i]]).reshape(-1, 1)\n\n        # Outer CV loop\n        skf = StratifiedKFold(n_splits=cv)\n        fold_aucs = []\n\n        for train_index, test_index in skf.split(X, y):\n            X_train, X_test = X[train_index], X[test_index]\n            y_train, y_test = y[train_index], y[test_index]\n\n            # Inner CV for hyperparameter tuning\n            grid_search = GridSearchCV(\n                LogisticRegression(solver='liblinear'),\n                param_grid,\n                cv=cv,\n                scoring='roc_auc'\n            )\n            grid_search.fit(X_train, y_train)\n\n            # Best logistic regression model\n            best_model = grid_search.best_estimator_\n\n            # Evaluate on the test set\n            y_pred = best_model.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]\n            fold_aucs.append(roc_auc_score(y_test, y_pred))\n\n        # Average AUC across folds\n        auc[i] = np.mean(fold_aucs)\n\n    return auc\n","numLines":584,"startLine":1,"totalLines":584}}}

  trial_labels, \n    cv = 20, \n    scale = False, \n    classifier = RidgeClassifierCV, \n    class_weight = 'balanced', \n    trial_min = 21, \n    train_min = 10,\n    test_min = 1,\n    lda_n_components = None,\n    random_seed = 42,\n    concat_folds = False,\n    return_scores = False,\n    keep_folds_separate = False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate AUC for a single neuron using classification.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n        fr_mean : array-like\n            Mean firing rates for each trial, shape (n_trials, n_neurons).\n        trial_labels : array-like\n            Labels for each trial, shape (n_trials,).\n        cv : int, optional\n            Number of cross-validation folds, by default 20.\n        scale : bool, optional\n            Whether to scale the firing rates, by default False.\n        classifier : class, optional\n            Classifier to use for classification, by default RidgeClassifierCV.\n        class_weight : str or dict, optional\n            Class weights for the classifier, by default 'balanced'.\n        trial_min : int, optional\n            Minimum number of trials per class for a valid split, by default 21.\n        train_min : int, optional\n            Minimum number of training trials per class, by default 10.\n        test_min : int, optional\n            Minimum number of testing trials per class, by default 1.\n        lda_n_components : int, optional\n            Number of components for LDA, by default None (not used).\n        random_seed : int, optional\n            Seed for random number generator, by default 42.\n        concat_folds : bool, optional\n            Whether to concatenate results across folds, by default False.\n        return_scores : bool, optional\n            Whether to return the scores for each fold, by default False.\n        keep_folds_separate : bool, optional\n            Whether to keep the folds separate in the output, by default False.\n    Returns\n    -------\n        auc_test : float or array\n            AUC for the test set, averaged across folds if `concat_folds` is False.\n        auc_train : float or array\n            AUC for the training set, averaged across folds if `concat_folds` is False.\n        test_scores : array, optional\n            Decision function scores for the test set, returned if `return_scores` is True.\n        train_scores : array, optional\n            Decision function scores for the training set, returned if `return_scores` is True.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _seed = random_seed\n\n    n_categories = len(np.unique(trial_labels))\n    if n_categories != 2:\n        raise ValueError('Only binary classification is supported for now. Number of categories: {}'.format(n_categories))\n\n    if np.sum(trial_labels == np.unique(trial_labels)[0]) < trial_min or np.sum(trial_labels == np.unique(trial_labels)[1]) < trial_min:\n        raise ValueError('At least one of the classes has fewer than {} trials'.format(trial_min))\n\n    not_valid_split = True\n    while not_valid_split:\n        split_inds_dict = random_split(trial_labels, cv = cv, random_seed = _seed)\n        not_valid_split = False\n        for ii in range(cv):\n            train_inds = split_inds_dict['train'][ii]\n            test_inds = split_inds_dict['test'][ii]\n            if np.sum(trial_labels[train_inds] == np.unique(trial_labels)[0]) < train_min or np.sum(trial_labels[train_inds] == np.unique(trial_labels)[1]) < train_min:\n                not_valid_split = True\n                _seed += 1\n            if np.sum(trial_labels[test_inds] == np.unique(trial_labels)[0]) < test_min or np.sum(trial_labels[test_inds] == np.unique(trial_labels)[1]) < test_min:\n                not_valid_split = True\n                _seed += 1\n\n    \n    model = classifier(class_weight = class_weight)\n    test_scores = []\n    train_scores = []\n\n    for ii in range(cv):\n        train_inds = split_inds_dict['train'][ii]\n        test_inds = split_inds_dict['test'][ii]\n\n        if scale:\n            scaler = StandardScaler()\n            fr_train = scaler.fit_transform(fr_mean[train_inds])\n            fr_test = scaler.transform(fr_mean[test_inds])\n        else:\n            fr_train = fr_mean[train_inds]\n            fr_test = fr_mean[test_inds]\n\n        model.fit(fr_train, trial_labels[train_inds])\n        \n        test_scores.append(model.decision_function(fr_test))\n        train_scores.append(model.decision_function(fr_train))\n\n    if concat_folds:\n        test_scores = np.concatenate(test_scores)\n        train_scores = np.concatenate(train_scores)\n        test_labels = np.concatenate([trial_labels[split_inds_dict['test'][ii]] for ii in range(cv)])\n        train_labels = np.concatenate([trial_labels[split_inds_dict['train'][ii]] for ii in range(cv)])\n\n        auc_test = roc_auc_score(test_labels, test_scores)\n        auc_train = roc_auc_score(train_labels, train_scores)\n\n    else:\n        if keep_folds_separate:\n            auc_test = np.array([roc_auc_score(trial_labels[split_inds_dict['test'][ii]], test_scores[ii]) for ii in range(cv)])\n            auc_train = np.array([roc_auc_score(trial_labels[split_inds_dict['train'][ii]], train_scores[ii]) for ii in range(cv)])\n        else:\n            auc_test = np.mean([roc_auc_score(trial_labels[split_inds_dict['test'][ii]], test_scores[ii]) for ii in range(cv)])\n            auc_train = np.mean([roc_auc_score(trial_labels[split_inds_dict['train'][ii]], train_scores[ii]) for ii in range(cv)])\n    \n    if return_scores:\n        return auc_test, auc_train, test_scores, train_scores\n    else:\n        return auc_test, auc_train\n    \ndef calculate_multi_neuron_auc(fr_mean, group1_labels, group2_labels, cv = 20, concat_folds = False, keep_folds_separate = False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate AUC for multiple neurons by performing classification on firing rates.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n        fr_mean : array-like\n            Mean firing rates for each trial, shape (n_trials, n_neurons).\n        group1_labels : array-like\n            Boolean array or indices for the first group of trials.\n        group2_labels : array-like\n            Boolean array or indices for the second group of trials.\n        cv : int, optional\n            Number of cross-validation folds, by default 20.\n        concat_folds : bool, optional\n            Whether to concatenate results across folds, by default False.\n        keep_folds_separate : bool, optional\n            Whether to keep the folds separate in the output, by default False.\n    Returns\n    -------\n        auc_array_test : array\n            AUC for the test set for each neuron, shape (n_neurons,) or (n_neurons, cv) if `keep_folds_separate` is True.\n        auc_array_train : array\n            AUC for the training set for each neuron, shape (n_neurons,) or (n_neurons, cv) if `keep_folds_separate` is True.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    n_neurons = fr_mean.shape[1]\n    fr1 = fr_mean[group1_labels]\n    fr2 = fr_mean[group2_labels]\n\n    _fr = np.concatenate([fr1, fr2], axis = 0)\n    _labels = np.concatenate([np.zeros(fr1.shape[0]), np.ones(fr2.shape[0])])\n\n    if keep_folds_separate:\n        auc_array_test = np.zeros((n_neurons, cv))\n        auc_array_train = np.zeros((n_neurons, cv))\n    else:\n        auc_array_test = np.zeros(n_neurons)\n        auc_array_train = np.zeros(n_neurons)\n\n    for ii in range(n_neurons):\n        auc_test, auc_train = calculate_single_neuron_auc_from_classification(_fr[:,ii].reshape(-1, 1), _labels, cv = cv, concat_folds = concat_folds, keep_folds_separate=keep_folds_separate)\n        auc_array_test[ii] = auc_test\n        auc_array_train[ii] = auc_train\n\n    return auc_array_test, auc_array_train","numLines":212,"startLine":1,"totalLines":212}}}

How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 50ms non-overlapping bins in a window of -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue (80 bins). What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the AI finds the last `sample_start_time` before the go cue using `searchsorted`. It computes the tone onset time relative to the go cue, then subtracts this from `bin_centers` to get time-from-tone-onset at each time bin.\n\nii.\n```python\nss_idx = np.searchsorted(sample_starts_all, go_time, side='right') - 1\nif ss_idx >= 0:\n    tone_onset_rel = sample_starts_all[ss_idx] - go_time\nelse:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85  # default fallback\ntime_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel\n```\n\niii. The AI justified using `searchsorted` to handle the fact that `sample_start_times` is a global array (not per-trial) and may include replayed epochs. The fallback of -1.85s is the expected default sample-to-go interval (650ms sample + 1200ms delay = 1850ms).\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input uses the same `bin_centers` array as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment. `bin_centers` are computed as the center of each 50ms bin within the [-2.5, 1.5]s window.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_centers = np.linspace(WINDOW_START + BIN_WIDTH/2, WINDOW_END - BIN_WIDTH/2, n_bins)\ntime_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel\ninput_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone.astype(np.float32), photostim_binary], axis=0)\n```\n\niii. By using `bin_centers` for both neural and input data, temporal alignment is guaranteed.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. Photostimulation is derived from `intervals/trials/photostim_onset` (onset time relative to trial start), `intervals/trials/photostim_duration` (duration in seconds), and `intervals/trials/start_time` (absolute trial start time).\n\nii.\n```python\nphotostim_onset_trial = trials['photostim_onset'][:]\nphotostim_duration_trial = trials['photostim_duration'][:]\ntrial_start_times = trials['start_time'][:]\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. For each trial with photostim (onset != 'N/A'), the AI converts the onset from trial-start-relative to go-cue-relative time, computes the end time using onset + duration, and creates a binary array indicating whether photostim is on at each bin center.\n\nii.\n```python\nphotostim_binary = np.zeros(n_bins, dtype=np.float32)\nps_onset_val = photostim_onset_trial[trial_idx]\nif ps_onset_val != b'N/A':\n    ps_onset_float = float(ps_onset_val)\n    ps_dur_float = float(photostim_duration_trial[trial_idx])\n    trial_start = trial_start_times[trial_idx]\n    ps_rel_start = (trial_start + ps_onset_float) - go_time\n    ps_rel_end = ps_rel_start + ps_dur_float\n    photostim_binary = ((bin_centers >= ps_rel_start) & (bin_centers < ps_rel_end)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 50ms non-overlapping bins in a window of -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue (80 bins). Spike counts are divided by bin width (0.05s) to get firing rates in Hz. For each trial, spikes within the window are found using `searchsorted`, aligned to go cue, and histogrammed.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_firing_rates_fast(spike_times_flat, spike_times_index, go_cue_times,\n                               good_indices, trial_indices, ...):\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(window_start, window_end, n_bins + 1)\n    for trial_idx in trial_indices:\n        go_time = go_cue_times[trial_idx]\n        fr_trial = np.zeros((n_good, n_bins), dtype=np.float32)\n        abs_start = go_time + window_start\n        abs_end = go_time + window_end\n        for i, unit_spikes in enumerate(good_spike_times):\n            idx_lo = np.searchsorted(unit_spikes, abs_start)\n            idx_hi = np.searchsorted(unit_spikes, abs_end)\n            if idx_hi > idx_lo:\n                aligned = unit_spikes[idx_lo:idx_hi] - go_time\n                counts = np.histogram(aligned, bins=bin_edges)[0]\n                fr_trial[i, :] = counts / bin_width\n        firing_rates_list.append(fr_trial)\n```\n\niii. The NWB `classification` field contains these labels directly.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to go cue onset. For each trial, the go cue time is obtained from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps`, and spikes are extracted in the window [-2.5, +1.5]s relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo_times = f['acquisition']['BehavioralEvents']['go_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n# In compute_firing_rates_fast:\ngo_time = go_cue_times[trial_idx]\nabs_start = go_time + window_start  # go_time - 2.5\nabs_end = go_time + window_end      # go_time + 1.5\naligned = unit_spikes[idx_lo:idx_hi] - go_time\n```\n\niii. The decoder task instructions specify \"Temporally align based on Go cue onset\" and \"Extract 2.5 s before to 1.5 s after the go cue.\" The AI follows this exactly.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is 50ms (0.05s). There are 80 bins covering the 4s window (-2.5 to +1.5s). No rebinning is applied; firing rates are computed directly from spike times using `np.histogram` with these bin edges.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05  # 50 ms bins\nWINDOW_START = -2.5  # seconds relative to go cue\nWINDOW_END = 1.5\nN_TIMEBINS = int(round((WINDOW_END - WINDOW_START) / BIN_WIDTH))  # 80 bins\n```\n\niii. The decoder task specifies \"Use 50-ms-width bins for computing firing rates.\" The reference code uses 40ms bins with 3.4ms stride, but the AI correctly follows the decoder task specification instead.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Time from tone onset is derived from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps` (absolute timestamps of all sample epoch onsets, including replays) and the go cue times.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_starts_all = f['acquisition']['BehavioralEvents']['sample_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI noted that `sample_start_times` contains all sample epoch starts including replays due to early licks. The relevant onset for each trial is the last sample start before the go cue.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the AI finds the last `sample_start_time` before the go cue using `searchsorted`. It computes the tone onset time relative to the go cue, then subtracts this from `bin_centers` to get time-from-tone-onset at each time bin.\n\nii.\n```python\nss_idx = np.searchsorted(sample_starts_all, go_time, side='right') - 1\nif ss_idx >= 0:\n    tone_onset_rel = sample_starts_all[ss_idx] - go_time\nelse:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85  # default fallback\ntime_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel\n```\n\niii. The AI justified using `searchsorted` to handle the fact that `sample_start_times` is a global array (not per-trial) and may include replayed epochs. The fallback of -1.85s is the expected default sample-to-go interval (650ms sample + 1200ms delay = 1850ms).\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input uses the same `bin_centers` array as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment. `bin_centers` are computed as the center of each 50ms bin within the [-2.5, 1.5]s window.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_centers = np.linspace(WINDOW_START + BIN_WIDTH/2, WINDOW_END - BIN_WIDTH/2, n_bins)\ntime_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel\ninput_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone.astype(np.float32), photostim_binary], axis=0)\n```\n\niii. By using `bin_centers` for both neural and input data, temporal alignment is guaranteed.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. Photostimulation is derived from `intervals/trials/photostim_onset` (onset time relative to trial start), `intervals/trials/photostim_duration` (duration in seconds), and `intervals/trials/start_time` (absolute trial start time).\n\nii.\n```python\nphotostim_onset_trial = trials['photostim_onset'][:]\nphotostim_duration_trial = trials['photostim_duration'][:]\ntrial_start_times = trials['start_time'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI identified that photostim onset is stored relative to trial start time, requiring conversion to go-cue-relative time.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. For each trial with photostim (onset != 'N/A'), the AI converts the onset from trial-start-relative to go-cue-relative time, computes the end time using onset + duration, and creates a binary array indicating whether photostim is on at each bin center.\n\nii.\n```python\nphotostim_binary = np.zeros(n_bins, dtype=np.float32)\nps_onset_val = photostim_onset_trial[trial_idx]\nif ps_onset_val != b'N/A':\n    ps_onset_float = float(ps_onset_val)\n    ps_dur_float = float(photostim_duration_trial[trial_idx])\n    trial_start = trial_start_times[trial_idx]\n    ps_rel_start = (trial_start + ps_onset_float) - go_time\n    ps_rel_end = ps_rel_start + ps_dur_float\n    photostim_binary = ((bin_centers >= ps_rel_start) & (bin_centers < ps_rel_end)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI verified that the absolute photostim time computed this way matches `photostim_start_times` events in the NWB file. Control trials (no photostim) have all-zero binary arrays.\n\n## 4-c. How is the `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The photostim binary array uses the same `bin_centers` as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment.\n\nii. Same `bin_centers` array is used:\n```python\nphotostim_binary = ((bin_centers >= ps_rel_start) & (bin_centers < ps_rel_end)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. Alignment is achieved by evaluating photostim timing at the same bin centers used for firing rate computation.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction choice* derived from?\n\ni. Lick direction choice is derived from `intervals/trials/trial_instruction`, which records the instructed lick direction ('left' or 'right') for each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_instruction = trials['trial_instruction'][:]\n# In output construction:\nchoice = 0 if trial_instruction[trial_idx] == b'left' else 1\n```\n\niii. The AI uses `trial_instruction` (the instructed/correct direction) rather than actual lick direction. This means for \"miss\" trials (incorrect licks), the recorded \"choice\" reflects what the mouse was supposed to do, not what it actually did.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction choice*?\n\ni. Left is encoded as 0, right as 1. The value is constant across all time bins for a given trial (per-trial output repeated across time).\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice = 0 if trial_instruction[trial_idx] == b'left' else 1\noutput_trial[0, :] = choice  # repeated across all time bins\n```\n\niii. The encoding matches the decoder task specification: \"left = 0, right = 1, per-trial.\"\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Outcome is derived from `intervals/trials/outcome`, which records the trial outcome as 'hit', 'miss', or 'ignore'.\n\nii.\n```python\noutcome = trials['outcome'][:]\nout = outcome[trial_idx]\nif out == b'ignore':\n    outcome_val = 0\nelif out == b'miss':\n    outcome_val = 1\nelif out == b'hit':\n    outcome_val = 2\nelse:\n    outcome_val = 0  # fallback to ignore\n```\n\niii. The AI maps outcome values to integers matching the decoder task specification: \"ignore = 0, miss = 1, hit = 2.\"\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. Outcome is encoded as a categorical integer (0=ignore, 1=miss, 2=hit). The value is constant across all time bins for a given trial. Any unexpected outcome values default to 0 (ignore).\n\nii.\n```python\noutput_trial[1, :] = outcome_val  # repeated across all time bins\n```\n\niii. The encoding matches the decoder task specification.\n\n## 6-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. There is no \"Distance to reward zone\" output in this dataset. The question appears to be from a template. The `Outcome` output is per-trial and constant across time, so alignment is trivial (the same value is replicated across all 80 time bins).\n\nii.\n```python\noutput_trial[1, :] = outcome_val  # same value for all time bins\n```\n\niii. Early lick is derived from `intervals/trials/early_lick`, which records whether the mouse licked early during the sample/delay epoch.\n\nii.\n```python\nearly_lick = trials['early_lick'][:]\nearly = 1 if early_lick[trial_idx] == b'early' else 0\n```\n\niii. Tongue y-position is derived from `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, which contains tracking data with columns for x-position, y-position, and likelihood (confidence), sampled at ~300 Hz.\n\nii.\n```python\nhas_tongue = 'Camera0_side_TongueTracking' in f['acquisition']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\nif has_tongue:\n    tongue_data = f['acquisition']['BehavioralTimeSeries']['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']['data'][:]\n    tongue_timestamps = f['acquisition']['BehavioralTimeSeries']['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']['timestamps'][:]\n```\n\niii. The raw tongue y-position (column index 1) is extracted and filtered by a likelihood threshold of 0.1 (column index 2). Only data points with likelihood > 0.1 are considered valid. The valid y-values are averaged within each 50ms time bin.\n\nii.\n```python\ny_slice = tongue_data[idx_start:idx_end, 1]\nlk_slice = tongue_data[idx_start:idx_end, 2]\nhigh_conf = lk_slice > 0.1\nfor b in range(n_bins):\n    bin_mask = (ts_slice >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_slice < bin_edges[b+1]) & high_conf\n    n_in_bin = np.sum(bin_mask)\n    if n_in_bin > 0:\n        tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_slice[bin_mask])\n```\n\niii. The likelihood threshold of 0.1 filters out low-confidence tracking data. Bins with no valid data get NaN values.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session percentiles (40th and 60th) are computed from ALL valid (non-NaN) tongue y values across all bins and all selected trials. Values below 40th percentile are category 0, between 40th and 60th are category 1, above 60th are category 2. NaN values (no valid tracking data) default to category 1 (middle).\n\nii.\n```python\nall_tongue_y_arr = np.array(all_tongue_y)\np40 = np.percentile(all_tongue_y_arr, 40)\np60 = np.percentile(all_tongue_y_arr, 60)\n\ntongue_y_disc = np.ones(n_bins, dtype=np.int64)  # default to 1 (middle)\nvalid_mask = ~np.isnan(tongue_y)\nif np.any(valid_mask):\n    tongue_y_disc[valid_mask & (tongue_y < p40)] = 0\n    tongue_y_disc[valid_mask & (tongue_y >= p40) & (tongue_y <= p60)] = 1\n    tongue_y_disc[valid_mask & (tongue_y > p60)] = 2\n```\n\niii. The AI follows the decoder task specification for discretization thresholds. The default of category 1 for NaN values is a design choice not specified in the instructions.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Tongue tracking data is binned using the same bin edges as the neural data (derived from the same window and bin width), ensuring temporal alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_edges = np.linspace(window_start, window_end, n_bins + 1)\nfor b in range(n_bins):\n    bin_mask = (ts_slice >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_slice < bin_edges[b+1]) & high_conf\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the neural data bins, ensuring the tongue y values are aligned in time.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several missing data scenarios are handled:\n- **Missing tongue tracking**: Sessions without `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` default to all-NaN tongue y values, which are then categorized as 1 (middle).\n- **Missing tone onset**: If no sample start time is found before the go cue (`ss_idx < 0`), a default of -1.85s is used.\n- **Partial neural recording**: Trials beyond the recorded range (`n_recorded_trials`) or with go cue times outside the spike time range are marked as invalid.\n- **Unexpected outcome values**: Any outcome not in {hit, miss, ignore} defaults to 0 (ignore).\n- **Sessions with too few trials**: Sessions with < 2 valid trials are skipped.\n- **Sessions with no good units**: Skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Missing tongue tracking\nif not has_tongue:\n    tongue_y_trials = [np.full(n_bins, np.nan) for _ in trial_indices]\n    all_tongue_y = []\n\n# Missing tone onset\nif ss_idx >= 0:\n    tone_onset_rel = sample_starts_all[ss_idx] - go_time\nelse:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85\n\n# Partial neural recording\nneural_valid = np.zeros(n_trials_total, dtype=bool)\nfor t_idx in range(min(n_trials_total, n_recorded_trials)):\n    go = go_times[t_idx]\n    if (go + WINDOW_START >= min_spike_time - 1.0 and\n        go + WINDOW_END <= max_spike_time + 1.0):\n        neural_valid[t_idx] = True\n```\n\niii. The AI documented these edge cases in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 10.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The firing rate computation is the most time-consuming step, taking ~3-14s per session. This involves nested loops over units and trials, with `searchsorted` and `np.histogram` operations for each.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Nested loop: for each trial, for each unit\nfor trial_idx in trial_indices:\n    for i, unit_spikes in enumerate(good_spike_times):\n        idx_lo = np.searchsorted(unit_spikes, abs_start)\n        idx_hi = np.searchsorted(unit_spikes, abs_end)\n        if idx_hi > idx_lo:\n            aligned = unit_spikes[idx_lo:idx_hi] - go_time\n            counts = np.histogram(aligned, bins=bin_edges)[0]\n            fr_trial[i, :] = counts / bin_width\n```\n\niii. The AI estimated ~7s average per session and ~16 minutes total for all sessions. The firing rate computation dominates.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Two key loops could be vectorized:\n1. **Firing rate computation**: The inner loop over units could be vectorized by concatenating all spikes with unit labels and using vectorized histogram operations.\n2. **Tongue y binning**: The per-bin loop in `get_tongue_y_for_trials` could be replaced with `np.digitize` or vectorized bin assignment.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Current: loop over bins for tongue y\nfor b in range(n_bins):\n    bin_mask = (ts_slice >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_slice < bin_edges[b+1]) & high_conf\n    if np.sum(bin_mask) > 0:\n        tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_slice[bin_mask])\n```\n\niii. The AI noted optimization with `searchsorted` for firing rates but did not fully vectorize the inner loops. The tongue y binning loop is particularly amenable to vectorization.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Several operations are repeated:\n1. **NWB file I/O**: Each session file is opened, read, and closed independently. No caching or parallel loading.\n2. **Bin edges computation**: `np.linspace(window_start, window_end, n_bins + 1)` is computed in both `compute_firing_rates_fast` and `get_tongue_y_for_trials`, though with identical results.\n3. **Bin centers computation**: Computed both in the main function and used implicitly in firing rate computation.\n\nii.\n```python\n# In compute_firing_rates_fast:\nbin_edges = np.linspace(window_start, window_end, n_bins + 1)\n# In get_tongue_y_for_trials:\nbin_edges = np.linspace(window_start, window_end, n_bins + 1)\n```\n\niii. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. control_non_early)\n    correct_right = np.sum((outcome == b'hit') & (trial_instruction == b'right') & control_non_early)\n    \n    print(f\"    Correct rate: {correct_rate:.2%}, correct L: {correct_left}, correct R: {correct_right}\")\n    \n    if correct_rate < MIN_CORRECT_RATE:\n        print(f\"    Skipping: correct rate too low\")\n        f.close()\n        return None\n    \n    if correct_left < MIN_CORRECT_TRIALS_PER_SIDE or correct_right < MIN_CORRECT_TRIALS_PER_SIDE:\n        print(f\"    Skipping: insufficient correct trials per side\")\n        f.close()\n        return None\n    \n    # ---- Unit filtering ----\n    classification = f['units']['classification'][:]\n    good_mask = classification == b'good'\n    n_good = int(np.sum(good_mask))\n    n_total = len(classification)\n    good_indices = np.where(good_mask)[0]\n    \n    print(f\"    Units: {n_total} total, {n_good} good\")\n    \n    if n_good == 0:\n        print(f\"    Skipping: no good units\")\n        f.close()\n        return None\n    \n    # ---- Brain regions ----\n    electrodes_idx = f['units']['electrodes'][:]\n    electrodes_index = f['units']['electrodes_index'][:]\n    electrode_locations = f['general']['extracellular_ephys']['electrodes']['location'][:]\n    \n    unit_regions = []\n    for i in range(n_total):\n        start = 0 if i == 0 else int(electrodes_index[i-1])\n        elec_idx = electrodes_idx[start]\n        region = extract_brain_region(electrode_locations[elec_idx])\n        unit_regions.append(region)\n    \n    good_regions = [unit_regions[i] for i in range(n_total) if good_mask[i]]\n    \n    t1 = time.time()\n    print(f\"    Data loading: {t1-t0:.1f}s\")\n    \n    # ---- Compute firing rates ----\n    firing_rates = {t2-t1:.1f}s\")\n    \n    # ---- Construct inputs ----\n    n_bins = N_TIMEBINS\n    bin_centers = np.linspace(WINDOW_START + BIN_WIDTH/2, WINDOW_END - BIN_WIDTH/2, n_bins)\n    \n    inputs_list = []\n    for trial_idx in trial_indices:\n        go_time = go_times[trial_idx]\n        \n        # Input 0: Time from tone onset\n        ss_idx = np.searchsorted(sample_starts_all, go_time, side='right') - 1\n        if ss_idx >= 0:\n            tone_onset_rel = sample_starts_all[ss_idx] - go_time\n        else:\n            tone_onset_rel = -1.85\n        time_from_tone = bin_centers - tone_onset_rel\n        \n        # Input 1: Photostim on/off\n        photostim_binary = np.zeros(n_bins, dtype=np.float32)\n        ps_onset_val = photostim_onset_trial[trial_idx]\n        if ps_onset_val != b'N/A':\n            ps_onset_float = float(ps_onset_val)\n            ps_dur_float = float(photostim_duration_trial[trial_idx])\n            trial_start = trial_start_times[trial_idx]\n            ps_rel_start = (trial_start + ps_onset_float) - go_time\n            ps_rel_end = ps_rel_start + ps_dur_float\n            photostim_binary = ((bin_centers >= ps_rel_start) & (bin_centers < ps_rel_end)).astype(np.float32)\n        \n        input_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone.astype(np.float32), photostim_binary], axis=0)\n        inputs_list.append(input_trial)\n    \n    t3 = time.time()\n    print(f\"    Input construction: {t3-t2:.1f}s\")\n    \n    # ---- Construct outputs ----\n    has_tongue = {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_7","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,260p' /app/methods.txt\"","aggregated_output":"Behavior and video tracking\n\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). The instruction stimuli during the sample epoch were pure tones played at one of two frequencies (3 kHz or 12 kHz). Each tone was played three times for 150 ms with 100 ms inter-tone intervals. The sample epoch was followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch. An auditory ‘Go’ cue (carrier frequency of 6 kHz with 360 Hz modulating fre-\nquency, 0.1 s duration) indicated the end of the delay epoch. Licking early during the sample/delay epoch triggered a replay of the epoch. During the response epoch (answer period: 1.5 s) mice reported the instruction by licking one of the two lick ports. Licking (consumption period: 1.5 s) the correct lick port triggered a small water reward (0.1 ~ 0.2 ul). Licking the incorrect lick port triggered a timeout (1-3 s). After mice stopped licking for 1.5 s, the trial ended, followed by a 250 ms inter-trial-interval. Early lick trials and no\nresponse trials were excluded for analysis. Overall performance was computed as the fraction of correct control trials (i.e. no photostimulation), excluding any early lick trials. We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each. Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%; STAR Methods). During the delay epoch, mice maintain a memory of their choice and plan a movement.\n\nTwo CMOS cameras (CM3-U3-13Y3M, FLIR) were used to track orofacial movements of the mouse under IR light illumination (940 nm LED). The cameras were equipped with 4-12 mm focal length lenses (12VM412ASIR, Tamron) and a pixel resolution of 71 mm. High-speed videos from a side view and a bottom view (Figure 1C) were acquired at 300 Hz using software FlyCapture (TELEDYNE FLIR). We trained DeepLabCut 39 to track the movement of tongue, jaw and nose (Figure 1I).\n\nMulti-regional electrophysiological recordings\n\nWe designed a ﬂexible manipulator system for multiple Neuropixels probe insertions (https://www.janelia.org/open-science/manipulator-system-for-multiple-neuropixels-probe-recordings). Three to four small craniotomies (diameter, 1 \u0018 1.5 mm) were made over target brain areas one day before the ﬁrst recording session. Details of recordings made with multiple Neuropixels probes in head-restrained behaving animals have been published (dx.doi.org/10.17504/protocols.io.8tphwmn). Target regions, insertions angles, probe types, etc. are summarized in Table S2. All coordinates are given with respect to Bregma (AP, anterior-posterior; ML, medial-lateral; DV, dorsal-ventral).\n\nExtracellular spikes were recorded using Neuropixels 1.0 probes, 2.0 single-shank (SS) and 2.0 multi-shank (MS) probes 3,4 (Table S2). Three to seven recordings were made from each craniotomy. Recording depth was inferred from manipulator readings (Sensapax uMp manipulators). After completion of probe insertion, brain tissue was allowed to settle for several minutes before recording. During recordings the craniotomies were immersed in saline.\n\nProbes were conﬁgured and data were visualized and streamed to disk using the open-source software package SpikeGLX (https://billkarsh.github.io/SpikeGLX/). Data from up to ﬁve simultaneously recorded probes were acquired at 30 kHz. Sync waves (0.5 s duty cycle TTL pulses) were recorded across probes and on auxiliary channels for synchronization.\n\nPhotoinhibition\n\nLight from two 473 nm lasers (Coherent OBIS LS/LX) was used to illuminate the two hemispheres of ALM. The laser power was controlled by analog outputs controlled by a Bpod (Sanworks). Photoinactivation of ALM (centered on AP 2.5 mm; ML 1.5 mm) was performed through clear-skull cap implant11 or via craniotomy by directing the laser over the skull (beam diameter, 1.8 mm at 4 sigma). Photoinhibition of the left-, right-ALM hemisphere, or both hemispheres was deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials 11,21 (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice). To prevent the mice from distinguishing photostimulation trials from control rials using visual cues, a ‘masking ﬂash’ (1 ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered using 470 nm LEDs in front of the mice throughout the entire trial. For silencing ALM, we stimulated cortical GABAergic neurons in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice. We used 40 Hz photostimulation with a sinusoidal temporal proﬁle (5 mW average power per hemisphere) and a 100 ms linear ramping down during the laser offset to reduce rebound neuronal activity. This photo stimulus is expected to silence glutamatergic neurons in all of ALM. 37 We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s), including the 100 ms ramp-down. Thus, photoinhibition always ended before the ‘Go’ cue.\n\nTo analyze the effects of photoinhibition, units with at least 10 photostimulation trials were selected for analysis (Figures 5 and S5). Bilateral ALM photostimulation reduced behavior performance from 83.2% to 71.7% (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice, n = 93 sessions). To exclude the effects of rebound population activity in the thalamus 16 and other subcortical regions, we used a 20-120 ms\ntime window after photo stimulation onset to measure the effect of ALM inactivation in downstream regions (Figures 5 and S5). For ALM itself, we used 500 ms (i.e. the entire inactivation period) to calculate spike rate reduction.\n\nSpike sorting and quality control\n\nWe developed a data pipeline for preprocessing, spike sorting and quality control (https://github.com/jenniferColonell/ecephys_spike_sorting). The extracellular recording traces were ﬁrst band-pass ﬁltered (Butterworth acausal ﬁlter, 250 Hz - 9 kHz). We then sorted the dataset using both Kilosort2 32 (MATLAB version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/KS20_for_preprocessed_data: Release version of KS2 for preprocessed data) and Kilosort2.5 4 (Python version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/pykilosort: [WIP] Python port of Kilosort 2). Both sorting methods gave similar results. In the analyses presented in the paper, we used the output from Kilosort2. Each output cluster was associated with a list of 15 quality metrics calculated to assess isolation and sorting quality 7 (i.e. quality control) (https://github.com/AllenInstitute/ecephys_spike_sorting/tree/master/ecephys_spike_sorting/modules/quality_metrics).\n\nThe details of the spike sorting and quality control are described in an accompanying white paper (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2. \n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Empty 0x1 cell. Need to manually define what it becomes in Python.\n2. 1x1 cell. Will be directly read as non-iterables such as str, int, float, etc. Need to manually convert it to an iterable with length 1.\n3. Nx1 cell (N >= 2). Usually it's fine to read and directly store it in Python.\n\nComment: in case 2, when using isinstance(data, data_type), if data could be a number, consider the possibility of data being either float or int, i.e. use\n    if isinstance(data, float) or isinstance(data, int):\n        do something\n\"\"\"\n\nplt.rcParams['pdf.fonttype'] = 42\nplt.rcParams['ps.fonttype'] = 42\n\n\ndef sliding_histogram(spikeTimes, begin_time, end_time, bin_width, stride, rate=True):\n    '''\n    Calculates the number of spikes for each unit in each sliding bin of width bin_width, strided by stride.\n    begin_time and end_time are treated as the lower- and upper- bounds for bin centers.\n    if rate=True, calculate firing rates instead of bin spikes.\n    '''\n    bin_begin_time = begin_time\n    bin_end_time = end_time\n    # This is to deal with cases where for e.g. (bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride is actually 43 but evaluates to 42.999999... due to numerical errors in floating-point arithmetic.\n    if np.allclose((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride, math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1):\n        n_bins =  math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+2\n    else:\n        n_bins = math.floor((bin_end_time-bin_begin_time)/stride)+1\n    binCenters = bin_begin_time + np.arange(n_bins)*stride\n\n    binIntervals = np.vstack((binCenters-bin_width/2, binCenters+bin_width/2)).T\n\n    # Count number of spikes for each sliding bin\n    binSpikes = np.asarray([[[np.sum(np.all([trial>=binInt[0], trial<binInt[1]], axis=0)) for binInt in binIntervals] for trial in unit]\n        for unit in spikeTimes]).swapaxes(0,-1)\n\n    if rate:\n        return binCenters, binSpikes/float(bin_width) # get firing rates\n    return binCenters, binSpikes\n\n\ndef process_one_sess(data_folder, file_path_list, bw, stride, begin_time=-3.0, end_time=3.5, save_folder=os.path.join(os.pardir, 'data_processed'), qc_mode='classifier'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Process all probe files in one session.\n\n    Args:\n        data_folder (str): the folder containing all data including QC neuron ID files.\n        file_path_list: list of full file paths of all probes in this session.\n        bw (float): in sec.\n        stride (float): in sec.\n        qc_mode (str): 'classifier' or 'old'.\n    \"\"\"\n    print(qc_mode)\n    if qc_mode == 'classifier':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'goodunits')\n        print(qc_folder)\n    elif qc_mode == 'old':\n        qc_folder = os.path.join(data_folder, 'DJ_GoodUnitsIdx_14regions_old_qc')\n    else:\n        raise ValueError('Wrong qc_mode! Must be \"classifier\" or \"old\".')\n    \n    assert os.path.exists(qc_folder)\n\n    spike_times_sess = []\n    unit_info_sess = []\n    unit_qc_sess_list = []\n    ccf_coordinate_sess = []\n    ccf_label_sess = []\n    ccf_unit_id_sess = []\n\n    # ----- 1. concatenate all neurons from all probes -----\n    for i_file, file_path in enumerate(sorted(file_path_list)):\n        filename = os.path.split(file_path)[-1]\n        name_list = filename[:-4].split('_') # ['map-export, 'SC038', '20191119', '115109', 's4', 'p1']\n        mouse = name_list[1] # 'SC038'\n        date = name_list[2] # '20191119'\n        number = name_list[3]\n        session = int(name_list[4][1:]) # 4\n        probe = int(name_list[5][1:]) # 1\n        sess_name = '%s_%s_%d' % (mouse, date, session)\n        sess_name_qc = '_'.join([mouse, date, number]) # 'SC038_20191119_115109'\n\n        save_folder_sess = os.path.join(save_folder, 'stride%d_bw%d'%(stride*1000, bw*1000), sess_name)\n        if not os.path.exists(save_folder_sess):\n            os.makedirs(save_folder_sess)\n\n        if os.path.exists(os.path.join(save_folder_sess, 'others_finished.txt')):\n            print('%s has been processed. Each element is a list of 0 (lick left) and 1 (lick right)\n        lick_times = sess_file['behavior_lick_times'] # lick_times[trial_id] is ndarray, each element is a list of lick times (need to subtract go cue time from it)\n        correctness = sess_file['behavior_report'] # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64. 1: correct or free water, 0: error, -1: no response\n        gocue_time = sess_file['task_cue_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is go cue time, second row is duration of response period\n        delay_time = sess_file['task_delay_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is delay time, second row is duration of delay period\n        sample_time = sess_file['task_sample_time'] # ndarray, (2,n_trials). First row is sample time, second row is duration of sample period\n        delay_period = delay_time[1,:] # only need duration of delay period\n        sample_period = sample_time[1,:] # only need duration of sample period\n        stimulation = sess_file['task_stimulation'] # ndarray, (n_trials,4). The 4 columns are [laser_power, stim_type('1', '2', or '6', with 1,2,6 being left/right/both ALM perturbation), laser_on_time, laser_off_time] (with reference to trial start time - need to subtract go cue time from it).\n        trial_type = sess_file['task_trial_type'] # ndarray, (1,n_trials), 'l' or 'r'\n\n        # align stimulation time and lick times to gocue_time=0 #\n        lick_time_cal = []\n        for i_trial, lick_times_trial in enumerate(lick_times):\n            gocue_time_trial = gocue_time[0, i_trial]\n            lick_time_cal.append(lick_times_trial - gocue_time_trial)\n            stimulation[i_trial, 2:] -= gocue_time_trial\n        lick_times = lick_time_cal\n\n        if i_file == 0:\n            ### only save behavior and task info for the first probe file ###\n            sess_dict = {}\n            sess_dict['sess_name'] = sess_name\n            sess_dict['auto_learn_trials'] = auto_learn_trials\n            sess_dict['early_lick_trials'] = early_lick_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\n            sess_dict['auto_water_trials'] = auto_water_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\n            sess_dict['free_water_trials'] = free_water_trials # ndarray, (n_trials,), int64\n            sess_dict['lick_directions'] = lick_directions # ndarray, (n_trials). An index of '42' from this session means the 2nd (42-10-30) unit from p3.\n            '''\n            temp_list = list(glob.glob(os.path.join(qc_folder, '{}*.mat'.format(sess_name_qc))))\n            assert len(temp_list) == 1\n            filepath_qc = temp_list[0]\n\n            qc_file = preprocessing_utils.loadmat(filepath_qc)\n            # idx_qc_dict = qc_file['Idx'] # dict. Keys are 'region_qc' (e.g. 'ALM_qc').
